cgroup\+\_\+path() takes a spin lock. It is good practice not to take spin locks within trace point handlers, as they are mostly hidden from normal view. As cgroup\+\_\+path() can take the kernfs\+\_\+rename\+\_\+lock spin lock, it is best to not call that function from the trace event handler.

Note:\+ trace\+\_\+cgroup\+\_\+\#\+\#type\#\+\#\+\_\+enabled() is a static branch that will only be set when the trace event is enabled.

\+\_\+\+\_\+cgroup\+\_\+task\+\_\+count -\/ count the number of tasks in a cgroup. The caller is responsible for taking the css\+\_\+set\+\_\+lock. @cgrp:\+ the cgroup in question

void cgroup1\+\_\+pidlist\+\_\+destroy\+\_\+all (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structcgroup}{cgroup}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{cgrp}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Used to destroy all pidlists lingering waiting for destroy timer. None should be left afterwards.

Function Description:\+ Destroys all pidlists associated with a cgroup. It schedules destruction work for each pidlist and then flushes the workqueue to ensure all are destroyed. Called during cgroup destruction. 

Definition at line \doxymbox{\hyperlink{cgroup-v1_8c_source_l00228}{228}} of file \doxymbox{\hyperlink{cgroup-v1_8c_source}{cgroup-\/v1.\+c}}.

\Hypertarget{cgroup-internal_8h_a407bbd40b7fbbc4d79ca23fea901520f}\index{cgroup-\/internal.h@{cgroup-\/internal.h}!cgroup1\_release\_agent@{cgroup1\_release\_agent}}

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{cgroup1\_release\_agent()}{cgroup1\_release\_agent()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{cgroup-internal_8h_a407bbd40b7fbbc4d79ca23fea901520f} 
void cgroup1\+\_\+release\+\_\+agent (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct work\+\_\+struct \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{work}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Notify userspace when a cgroup is released, by running the configured release agent with the name of the cgroup (path relative to the root of cgroup file system) as the argument.

Most likely, this user command will try to rmdir this cgroup.

This races with the possibility that some other task will be attached to this cgroup before it is removed, or that some other user task will \textquotesingle{}mkdir\textquotesingle{} a child cgroup of this cgroup. That\textquotesingle{}s ok. The presumed \textquotesingle{}rmdir\textquotesingle{} will fail quietly if this cgroup is no longer unused, and this cgroup will be reprieved from its death sentence, to continue to serve a useful existence. Next time it\textquotesingle{}s released, we will get notified again, if it still has \textquotesingle{}notify\+\_\+on\+\_\+release\textquotesingle{} set.

The final arg to call\+\_\+usermodehelper() is UMH\+\_\+\+WAIT\+\_\+\+EXEC, which means only wait until the task is successfully execve()\textquotesingle{}d. The separate release agent task is forked by call\+\_\+usermodehelper(), then control in this thread returns here, without waiting for the release agent task. We don\textquotesingle{}t bother to wait because the caller of this routine has no use for the exit status of the release agent task, so no sense holding our caller up for that.

Function Description:\+ Work function for the cgroup1 release agent. It builds the command line with the release agent path and cgroup path, then executes it using call\+\_\+usermodehelper().

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{cgroup\_attach\_lock()}{cgroup\_attach\_lock()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{cgroup-internal_8h_a5d2da8f123e25ef75ad6aadb0252b294} 
void cgroup\+\_\+attach\+\_\+lock (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{enum \doxymbox{\hyperlink{cgroup-defs_8h_a6f88470278a2f9e2137cf0f8b6672f4a}{cgroup\+\_\+attach\+\_\+lock\+\_\+mode}}}]{lock\+\_\+mode}{, }\item[{struct task\+\_\+struct \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{tsk}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

cgroup\+\_\+attach\+\_\+lock -\/ Lock for -\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}attach() @lock\+\_\+mode:\+ whether acquire and acquire which rwsem @tsk:\+ thread group to lock

cgroup migration sometimes needs to stabilize threadgroups against forks and exits by write-\/locking cgroup\+\_\+threadgroup\+\_\+rwsem. However, some -\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}attach() implementations (e.\+g. cpuset), also need to disable CPU hotplug. Unfortunately, letting -\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}attach() operations acquire cpus\+\_\+read\+\_\+lock() can lead to deadlocks.

Bringing up a CPU may involve creating and destroying tasks which requires read-\/locking threadgroup\+\_\+rwsem, so threadgroup\+\_\+rwsem nests inside cpus\+\_\+read\+\_\+lock(). If we call an -\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}attach() which acquires the cpus lock while write-\/locking threadgroup\+\_\+rwsem, the locking order is reversed and we end up waiting for an on-\/going CPU hotplug operation which in turn is waiting for the threadgroup\+\_\+rwsem to be released to create new tasks. For more details:\+

\href{http://lkml.kernel.org/r/20220711174629.uehfmqegcwn2lqzu@wubuntu}{\texttt{http:\+/\+/\+lkml.\+kernel.\+org/\+r/\+20220711174629.\+uehfmqegcwn2lqzu@wubuntu}}

Resolve the situation by always acquiring cpus\+\_\+read\+\_\+lock() before optionally write-\/locking cgroup\+\_\+threadgroup\+\_\+rwsem. This allows -\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}attach() to assume that CPU hotplug is disabled on entry.

When favordynmods is enabled, take per threadgroup rwsem to reduce overhead on dynamic cgroup modifications. see the comment above CGRP\+\_\+\+ROOT\+\_\+\+FAVOR\+\_\+\+DYNMODS definition.

tsk is not NULL only when writing to cgroup.\+procs.

Function Description:\+ Acquires locks for cgroup attach operations. It takes cpus\+\_\+read\+\_\+lock() and optionally write-\/locks cgroup\+\_\+threadgroup\+\_\+rwsem or per-\/threadgroup rwsem. This prevents CPU hotplug and threadgroup changes during migration.

Function Description:\+ Shows the CPU time statistics for a cgroup in a seq\+\_\+file. For non-\/root cgroups, it flushes the stats, acquires the lock, reads the bstat, and adjusts the cputime. For the root cgroup, it calls \doxylink{rstat_8c_a7dc5136d6a692c1fe9c6a8f2ef766d81}{root\+\_\+cgroup\+\_\+cputime()}. It then prints usage, user, system, and nice time in microseconds, along with force idle time if configured.

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{cgroup-internal_8h_a3f6b206b0853d5e2ab585d01f6d92351} 
struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structcgroup}{cgroup}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} cgroup\+\_\+kn\+\_\+lock\+\_\+live (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct kernfs\+\_\+node \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{kn}{, }\item[{bool}]{drain\+\_\+offline}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

cgroup\+\_\+kn\+\_\+lock\+\_\+live -\/ locking helper for cgroup kernfs methods @kn:\+ the kernfs\+\_\+node being serviced @drain\+\_\+offline:\+ perform offline draining on the cgroup

This helper is to be used by a cgroup kernfs method currently servicing @kn. It breaks the active protection, performs cgroup locking and verifies that the associated cgroup is alive. Returns the cgroup if alive; otherwise, NULL. A successful return should be undone by a matching \doxylink{cgroup_8c_a5808e21adbb735b336c1ac679347c4f4}{cgroup\+\_\+kn\+\_\+unlock()} invocation. If @drain\+\_\+offline is true, the cgroup is drained of offlining csses before return.

Any cgroup kernfs method implementation which requires locking the associated cgroup should use this helper. It avoids nesting cgroup locking under kernfs active protection and allows all kernfs operations including self-\/removal.

Function Description:\+ Locks a cgroup\textquotesingle{}s kernfs node and returns the cgroup if it\textquotesingle{}s live (online). It breaks active protection, acquires cgroup\+\_\+mutex, and verifies the cgroup is not dead. Returns the cgroup or NULL.

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{cgroup-internal_8h_a91d7710c20f468bb8371512b15737bc2} 
int cgroup\+\_\+migrate (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct task\+\_\+struct \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{leader}{, }\item[{bool}]{threadgroup}{, }\item[{struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structcgroup__mgctx}{cgroup\+\_\+mgctx}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{mgctx}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

cgroup\+\_\+migrate -\/ migrate a process or task to a cgroup @leader:\+ the leader of the process or the task to migrate @threadgroup:\+ whether @leader points to the whole process or a single task @mgctx:\+ migration context

Migrate a process or task denoted by @leader. If migrating a process, the caller must be holding cgroup\+\_\+threadgroup\+\_\+rwsem. The caller is also responsible for invoking \doxylink{cgroup_8c_ae1c005f2422d148d0359a2032fb9e291}{cgroup\+\_\+migrate\+\_\+add\+\_\+src()} and \doxylink{cgroup_8c_a10644a3466731282fcec4423bbad0343}{cgroup\+\_\+migrate\+\_\+prepare\+\_\+dst()} on the targets before invoking this function and following up with \doxylink{cgroup_8c_aa7e924fc6912f50590ab05ee0f7bb029}{cgroup\+\_\+migrate\+\_\+finish()}.

As long as a controller\textquotesingle{}s -\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}can\+\_\+attach() doesn\textquotesingle{}t fail, this function is guaranteed to succeed. This means that, excluding -\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}can\+\_\+attach() failure, when migrating multiple targets, the success or failure can be decided for all targets by invoking group\+\_\+migrate\+\_\+prepare\+\_\+dst() before actually starting migrating.

Function Description:\+ Migrates a process or task to a cgroup. It collects tasks from the thread group and executes the migration. Returns 0 on success or a negative error code.

cgroup\+\_\+migrate\+\_\+add\+\_\+src -\/ add a migration source \doxylink{structcss__set}{css\+\_\+set} @src\+\_\+cset:\+ the source \doxylink{structcss__set}{css\+\_\+set} to add @dst\+\_\+cgrp:\+ the destination cgroup @mgctx:\+ migration context

Tasks belonging to @src\+\_\+cset are about to be migrated to @dst\+\_\+cgrp. Pin @src\+\_\+cset and add it to @mgctx-\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}src\+\_\+csets, which should later be cleaned up by \doxylink{cgroup_8c_aa7e924fc6912f50590ab05ee0f7bb029}{cgroup\+\_\+migrate\+\_\+finish()}.

This function may be called without holding cgroup\+\_\+threadgroup\+\_\+rwsem even if the target is a process. Threads may be created and destroyed but as long as cgroup\+\_\+mutex is not dropped, no new \doxylink{structcss__set}{css\+\_\+set} can be put into play and the preloaded css\+\_\+sets are guaranteed to cover all migrations.

Tasks are about to be moved and all the source css\+\_\+sets have been preloaded to @mgctx-\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}preloaded\+\_\+src\+\_\+csets. This function looks up and pins all destination css\+\_\+sets, links each to its source, and append them to @mgctx-\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}preloaded\+\_\+dst\+\_\+csets.

cgroup\+\_\+migrate\+\_\+vet\+\_\+dst -\/ verify whether a cgroup can be migration destination @dst\+\_\+cgrp:\+ destination cgroup to test

On the default hierarchy, except for the mixable, (possible) thread root and threaded cgroups, subtree\+\_\+control must be zero for migration destination cgroups with tasks so that child cgroups don\textquotesingle{}t compete against tasks.

Function Description:\+ Verifies that a cgroup can be a migration destination. On the default hierarchy, it checks the no-\/internal-\/process constraint. Returns 0 if valid or a negative error code.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{cgroup\_on\_dfl()}{cgroup\_on\_dfl()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{cgroup-internal_8h_aa8e9c07d7c652eb98e9053203c68b444} 
bool cgroup\+\_\+on\+\_\+dfl (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structcgroup}{cgroup}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{cgrp}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

cgroup\+\_\+on\+\_\+dfl -\/ test whether a cgroup is on the default hierarchy @cgrp:\+ the cgroup of interest

The default hierarchy is the v2 interface of cgroup and this function can be used to test whether a cgroup is on the default hierarchy for cases where a subsystem should behave differently depending on the interface version.

List of changed behaviors:\+


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item Mount options "{}noprefix"{}, "{}xattr"{}, "{}clone\+\_\+children"{}, "{}release\+\_\+agent"{} and "{}name"{} are disallowed.
\item When mounting an existing superblock, mount options should match.
\item rename(2) is disallowed.
\item "{}tasks"{} is removed. Everything should be at process granularity. Use "{}cgroup.\+procs"{} instead.
\item "{}cgroup.\+procs"{} is not sorted. pids will be unique unless they got recycled in-\/between reads.
\item "{}release\+\_\+agent"{} and "{}notify\+\_\+on\+\_\+release"{} are removed. Replacement notification mechanism will be implemented.
\item "{}cgroup.\+clone\+\_\+children"{} is removed.
\item "{}cgroup.\+subtree\+\_\+populated"{} is available. Its value is 0 if the cgroup and its descendants contain no task; otherwise, 1. The file also generates kernfs notification which can be monitored through poll and \+[di]\+notify when the value of the file changes.
\item cpuset:\+ tasks will be kept in empty cpusets when hotplug happens and take masks of ancestors with non-\/empty cpus/\+mems, instead of being moved to an ancestor.
\item cpuset:\+ a task can be moved into an empty cpuset, and again it takes masks of ancestors.
\item blkcg:\+ blk-\/throttle becomes properly hierarchical.
\end{DoxyItemize}

Function Description:\+ Tests whether a cgroup is on the default (v2) hierarchy. Returns true if the cgroup\textquotesingle{}s root is the default root. This is used to determine which behavior and features apply to the cgroup. 

Definition at line \doxymbox{\hyperlink{cgroup_8c_source_l00385}{385}} of file \doxymbox{\hyperlink{cgroup_8c_source}{cgroup.\+c}}.

cgroup\+\_\+subsys\+\_\+enabled() can only be used with literal subsys names which is fine for individual subsystems but unsuitable for cgroup core. This is slower static\+\_\+key\+\_\+enabled() based test indexed by @ssid.

Function Description:\+ Checks if a cgroup subsystem with the given ID is enabled. It uses the static key associated with the subsystem to determine if it\textquotesingle{}s available. This is slower than the static key API but works with subsystem IDs.

ss\+\_\+rstat\+\_\+init -\/ subsystem-\/specific rstat initialization @ss:\+ target subsystem

If @ss is NULL, the static locks associated with the base stats are initialized. If @ss is non-\/\+NULL, the subsystem-\/specific locks are initialized.

Function Description:\+ Initializes subsystem-\/specific rstat structures. It allocates a per-\/\+CPU llist head and initializes the subsystem\textquotesingle{}s rstat spinlock. If ss is NULL, it initializes the global base stats lock. This is called during subsystem registration.

Return the cgroup for "{}task"{} from the given hierarchy. Must be called with css\+\_\+set\+\_\+lock held to prevent task\textquotesingle{}s groups from being modified. Must be called with either cgroup\+\_\+mutex or rcu read lock to prevent the cgroup root from being destroyed.

Function Description:\+ Returns the cgroup of a task on a specific hierarchy. Must be called with css\+\_\+set\+\_\+lock held and either cgroup\+\_\+mutex or RCU read lock. This is used to find which cgroup a task belongs to.

\doxysubsection{Variable Documentation}
\Hypertarget{cgroup-internal_8h_a0dd991d8304cfda1ca8c377a36cb8582}\index{cgroup-\/internal.h@{cgroup-\/internal.h}!cgroup1\_base\_files@{cgroup1\_base\_files}}
\index{cgroup1\_base\_files@{cgroup1\_base\_files}!cgroup-\/internal.h@{cgroup-\/internal.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{cgroup1\_base\_files}{cgroup1\_base\_files}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{cgroup-internal_8h_a0dd991d8304cfda1ca8c377a36cb8582} 
struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structcftype}{cftype}} cgroup1\+\_\+base\+\_\+files\+[$\,$]\+\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [extern]}}

cgroup core interface files for the legacy hierarchies

Variable Description:\+ This is an array of cftype structures defining the core interface files for cgroup1 (legacy) hierarchies. Each entry represents a file that appears in cgroup directories, with handlers for reading and writing. The files include "{}cgroup.\+procs"{} (for moving thread groups), "{}tasks"{} (for moving individual tasks), "{}notify\+\_\+on\+\_\+release"{} (control release notifications), "{}release\+\_\+agent"{} (path to the release agent program, only on root), "{}cgroup.\+clone\+\_\+children"{} (cpuset cloning behavior), and "{}cgroup.\+sane\+\_\+behavior"{} (indicates legacy mode). The array is terminated by an empty entry.
